ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u13	Thank you, sir. Minister, you're welcome. Mr Marcin Horała.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u14	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Members, because here a large group of Members are asking for an increase in airport charges. Airport charges are those that impose airport managers on the airline, most of which are self-government airports, and the Ministry, frankly, does not have any knowledge about the plans to manage these airports. I suppose this isn't about airport charges, it's about navigation, route and terminal charges. These charges are set in a procedure which is uniform for all European Union countries, the general assumption of which is in terms of airspace control costs, i.e. a service which is carried out for carriers which are necessary for their business to be able to run their business, carriers must earn and must have such an airspace control service, but on the other hand it is not possible to organise airspace control differently than by one State institution. It cannot be imagined in the form of some private service, where there would be several competing controllers in the same airspace. So the carriers generate a certain cost by running their business, and this cost should be borne by them. Due to the crisis in the post-COVID aviation market, the number of air operations decreased. The Polish Air Navigation Agency has carried out a whole range of cost reduction measures on its side, thanks to which PLN 1 billion will be saved in this several-year perspective. But these actions have a natural barrier, i.e. airspace control must be ensured and this generates certain costs below which it cannot be achieved. In fact, the mathematical effect is that if a certain cost is divided into fewer operations, the cost of the operation is higher and therefore proposed in the context of the negotiations on the update of the plan of effective action of the increase in terminal and en route charges. I would like to point out that such increases are taking place throughout Europe. The highest increases are in Germany - 150% as regards the proposed increases in terminal charges at this stage of PSD consultation. In the next stage, once the Polish proposal is submitted, the Performance Review Body will be evaluated. This is an institution affiliated by the European Commission, which is about to examine whether it is true that these principles have been respected, whether they are real costs or not, whether they have been underestimated or whether some hidden subsidy has been made here. It shall approve this performance plan. Therefore, heartily regretting that such increases are, we must also be aware of their scale. If we are talking about a 70% increase for regional ports, this is an increase of several hundred zlotys per operation, i.e. per seat, per ticket a little bit it will vary depending on the aircraft, because there are different planes, different number of seats, it will be between 3 PLN and a maximum of 5 PLN, probably most often approx. 4 PLN, assuming that the carrier will want to transfer these costs for the price of the ticket. As I say, it is regrettable and we would like to avoid it, but unfortunately the reality is what they are and the math is what it is, the cost of airspace control is what they are. The direct grant proposed by your Members to PAZP is, of course, legally possible, albeit extremely rarely applied. Eurocontrol recommends credit solutions. Eurocontrol has also drawn up a commercial loan which offers individual airspace control agencies in individual countries so that they can refinance themselves. In Poland, Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego managed to provide the Polish Air Navigation Agency with a loan on better terms than that proposed by Eurocontrol. This is just one of the support mechanisms that PAZP received. However, once again at the end of the day the costs are what they are and others do not want to be, the number of air operations is the same. If these increases were excessive, they were not excessive, but if they were excessive, then after the next PAZP settlement period he would simply have to repay the funds, he would have to reduce the levy to the next settlement period in order to compensate for it. So once again, this is not malice, this is not a government's willful decision, this is the same procedure as in other countries of the European Union. These are the costs that are necessary to secure a peaceful flight and air traffic control, and these are the natural costs associated with the operation of carriers. When someone conducts their business on the Polish market, it generates costs determined by their business and unfortunately has to bear them. That's just the way it is. These costs are relatively high, although not the highest in Europe and not the highest in Central and Eastern Europe, and the scale of increases, the en route charges are even below the European average. It follows that we have in Poland a very scattered model of air transport, air infrastructure. This model was supported until 2015. We know what this was all about, about supporting the penetration of the Polish market by German hubs, by low cost lines, and lowering the value of the Polish national carrier, so that LOT will probably privatize somewhere in the plan at the end for a fraction of its potential value. But what's left of it is that we have this scattered model, which again generates costs, because the airspace control tower has to function at the airport. Even if it is a very small airport with a very small number of operations, the tower still has to be there and generate certain costs. However, even in this zone Poland 2, regional airport zone, terminal charges will be lower than e.g. in Romania or Hungary, not to mention Germany, where they will be much larger. If we are talking about grants, this solution has only occurred in four European countries, of which in three European Unions and Switzerland, three European countries in 27 have decided to do so. Among these countries are Germany, which raised the fees much more than in Poland, because by 150%. There is also Switzerland, which has raised fees more than in Poland. And finally, it's over. I would definitely not recall the Spanish case, because in Spain the airspace control agency (the bell) actually received support, but not from the government, but from airports. It's the airports that add directly to the airspace agency. I think in Poland airports would not want to add as much as in Spain. Thank you very much.

The Government shall take a number of measures concerning both the eradication of African swine fever and financial assistance to farms. These activities include: the reduction of the feral population by both hunting and, in addition, the sanitary discharge launched in counties where more than 150 000 are kept. pigs, remove potential sources of ASF virus from the environment as part of active search for dead feral pigs or their remains, conduct monitoring of feral pigs and pigs for early detection of infection and rapid implementation of measures to interrupt and eradicate the spread chain, rapid elimination of emerging outbreaks ASF in pigs and contact outbreaks, bioinsurance of pig holding holdings, activities or investments for the prevention, control and eradication of ASF in pigs and wild animals, including the reduction of fresh ASF cases, fragrance barriers, closing of crossings for animals on express roads and motorways, the execution of headhunting and information activities carried out by agricultural advisory centres. A few words about aid measures for pig producers. The Ministry has prepared the first integrated farm reconstruction programme after ASF. The aid under the scheme shall include instruments to ensure the implementation of measures relating to the bioinsurance of holdings, the elimination of the lowest buying-in price in ASF zones, the cover of civil law obligations entered into, the reconstruction of pig population, support for processing and shortening the supply chain. Currently, the programme is focused on investments in bioinsurance of pig holdings. The call for proposals is ongoing under Action 5.1. Completion of European Regional Development Fund (ERDF) - Objective 1 (prior to 2000) Aid takes the form of reimbursement of part of the eligible costs of operations - up to 80% - in the case of e.g. the creation or modernisation of a small disinfectant, the modernisation of buildings or the form of standard unit rates for the implementation of an investment concerning the execution of a pigsty fence. By 19 September 7680 aid applications were submitted for operations to prevent the spread of ASF, of which 4581 applications were contracted for PLN 214.5 million. Moreover, during the period of 6 August 2011 until 27 August 2011, pig producers could apply for aid as de minimis aid in agriculture for a refund of 50% expenditure incurred on the bioinsurance of holdings. The scheme also focuses on aid to compensate for the amount of reduced income obtained by the pig producer from the sale of pigs held in the restricted area in connection with the eradication of ASF. From 28 August to 30 November 2021 recruitment shall be carried out for quarterly periods and from 1 October to 30 November for annual periods. We also focus on providing financial cover for civil law obligations. Agricultural producers raising or breeding pigs in areas with restrictions due to occurrence ASF may apply for interest-free loans to finance outstanding civil liability. In addition, pig producers who, in view of the current ban on the holding of pigs or their introduction into the holding, have ceased their production may benefit from interest rates on loans. Producers of pigs who have ceased production in connection with the existing ban on the maintenance of pigs or their introduction into the holding may also benefit from financial assistance. The amount of aid shall be the product of the average annual number of pigs kept on the holding during the 12 months preceding the month in which the ban was introduced (the Bell) and of the amount of PLN 0,36 and the number of days in the calendar year in question. I would also like to add that there are funds for compensation, we pay them regularly when farmers meet the conditions laid down by veterinary medicine. We also provided assistance in relation to possibilities, with the presentation of COVID-19. The government is also going to... Thank you, Minister. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u48	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In answer to the question about inflation, of course, it is worth to start with such a fairly obvious statement, but it is always good to remind you that inflation often has a universal, social, much stronger dimension than macroeconomic factors show. And of course, the government must fully respect and respect these social feelings. On the other hand, it is impossible to escape certain macroeconomic comparisons and it is worth moving on to them. Firstly, the main risk we see at this point in terms of inflation is the risk of anchoring high inflation expectations, the so-called increased inflation expectations. the result of the second round, i.e. the driving mechanism for waiting for increases, whether it's pensions or salaries connected with the social feeling of inflation growth, so-called. anchoring the inflation target. At this point, the main task whether it's a central bank or a government policy, whether it's really all public stage players, it's quite reliable and real information about the actual size of current inflation. It is also worth paying attention to the causes of current inflationary readings. These causes are several and it is difficult to indicate which of them is actually the most important, so I will exchange them, but please do not treat them in such a way that the ones that will be mentioned first are crucial. Firstly, inflation, and this is a factor known from many historical studies. It appears very often at a time of quite strong economic growth, quite strong post-crisis reflection, which is linked to a very heated economy that is reborn after the economic crisis. Some side-effects are increased inflation readings. Secondly, what is happening in international markets, namely the rise in energy prices that are absolutely unrelated to government policy, the rise in ETS emissions certificates prices, the rise in commodity prices also related to political or geopolitical factors, a clear example of which is the increase in gas prices caused on the one hand by Russia's geopolitical policy, but on the other hand by some trends in the oil market, because given oil prices, we could say today that the oil supply alone could be much greater if not for example. The United States limits oil shale production. For the past few years, this has increased or stopped the rise in oil prices. When the price of oil per barrel reached about $60.70, the profitability of oil shale production in the United States was always restored at this point. There are no such phenomena now for some reasons related to climate policy. Turning to the issue of energy prices, it is worth paying attention to some factors that exist in China related to the lack of coal supply. Why am I talking about this? I am talking about this because, firstly, to point out that a lot of inflation factors are out of government control, and in fact these are global factors, secondly, to point out that this is a global trend also related to certain issues that are not affected, while it is worth noting that many of these countries are not reacting in a drastic way, reducing this inflation - China, the United States, Germany. It is worth mentioning here Mrs Lagarde's recent statement by the ECB's head, in which she stated that the temporary increase in inflation should not constitute a drastic tightening of monetary policy. It is also worth paying attention to a historical context. Namely, in the question it was stated that this inflation has been highest for 20 years, but as of May 2011, it is worth noting that inflation was then 5% So the reading is very similar to what we have today. What were the factors of inflation at the time? Very similar to those that occur now: above all, high prices of agricultural and energy raw materials in international markets, a significant depreciation of gold mainly due to a significant deterioration of the mood in global financial markets and - attention - a significant increase in VAT rates in 2011. This inflation has continued for several months, while in four years it has developed into deflation, which is long-term much worse for the economy than inflation. Why am I talking about this? Firstly, this is a proof that inflation is a cyclical, economical factor, which is clearly shown in 2011. Secondly, it may be a temporary factor. It is worth noting that current inflation does not have the signs of stagflation, which is probably the worst possible phenomenon, and this is confirmed by the current low reading on the unemployment rate, as well as the real increase in the salary that exceeds inflation. This is proof that fortunately there are no signs of stagflation factors. I've got 30 minutes left. What action can be taken to this end? The first natural action is, of course, the action concerning interest rates, of which it is not appropriate for me to speak for obvious reasons, as this is a sovereign policy of the NBP. The second action that is already taking place on the Polish market is the introduction of the so-called. tapering, i.e. limiting the quantitative relaxation mechanism used by central banks. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u66	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The questions asked by your Members indicate a lack of knowledge about the procedures, the rules in force concerning the appointment of members of supervisory boards and boards of companies with the State Treasury. I'll try to explain systematically what this process looks like. Perhaps the lack of knowledge is due to the fact that the Law on Property Management, which properly regulated this issue, was not adopted until 2016. If this legislation has not been noticed, then I serve as an explanation. Let's start with the board of supervisors. Only a person who has a positive opinion of the Company Board can be a candidate of the Treasury for this body. The council consists of people with a well-established professional, official and scientific career in various fields, such as law, finance, economics. Before the reform, the Treasury Minister decided to elect candidates for the Supervisory Board. The candidate must also comply with a number of statutory requirements. He must have adequate knowledge, experience, higher education and at least five years' work experience. In addition, he must pass a very difficult professional examination or have a doctorate degree in legal, economic, technical sciences, an appropriate professional title such as legal counsel, lawyer, statutory auditor, or a renowned international certificate such as CFA or ACCA, possibly he must complete an MBA programme in order to be able to sit in the composition of supervisory boards of companies with the participation of the State Treasury. And a word of comment about supervisory boards or people who meet these criteria - let's say that in some companies of significant importance these criteria are even more stringent. In addition to formal requirements, the Board of Companies with the participation of the State Treasury shall also verify the substantive preparation of the candidate for a member of the Supervisory Board. Therefore, the selection of candidates is made from persons who have high qualifications and the substantive knowledge needed in a particular company's supervisory board. However, this is not the end of the trial. The final decision on the appointment of the person to the supervisory board is taken in the vast majority of cases at the general meeting of the shareholders of the company concerned. I note that in many companies, especially the most important ones, the State Treasury has a share of less than 50%. Often this is the ceiling resulting from the Public Offering Act, i.e. the ceiling up to 33%. This means that at the general meeting it is also important to have the voice of shareholders other than the Treasury who may not agree to the candidacy submitted to the Supervisory Board. The exception is, of course, the statutory powers of the State Treasury in certain companies, which were frequently entered during their privatisation, before 2016. In such situations the appointment may take place by a declaration of the representative of the State Treasury, in some situations also without a resolution of the general meeting. About the board of directors. In accordance with the 2016 Law on the Principles for the Management of Property, which I have already mentioned, this issue has been settled. Members of the Boards of Companies with the State Treasury shall be appointed by means of a selection procedure. Such conduct is carried out by the Supervisory Board, which checks and evaluates the qualifications of the candidates and selects the best of them. In addition, in the financial sector, some of the members of the Management Board must be accepted by the Financial Supervision Authority. Ex ante is about the president and vice president of risk issues. If we are talking about an ex post procedure, it concerns all other members of the Management Board. It is also worth pointing out that, in accordance with the rules of either the supervisory board or the board, it is not possible to select persons who are assistants to Members or Senators or are part of a party or work for political parties. Finally, I would also like to point out that the list of all members of the supervisory boards of companies with the participation of the Treasury in the supervision of the Minister for State Assets is publicly available. So we have full transparency and substantive selection of staff. These standards are functioning now. But I can say that we are not resting on laurels, because the Sejm has just been visited by a project developed by the Ministry of State Assets, which has included a comprehensive, largest trade law reform in 20 years. One of the two pillars of the proposed amendments is to increase the effectiveness of supervisory oversight by supervisory boards. This does not apply to supervisory boards of State Treasury companies, but to all entities. We are giving these councils new competences, but there is also greater responsibility behind them. Thank you, Marshal. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u86	Mr. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Ladies and gentlemen! The consumption of energy drinks, of course, is a big problem, and it is starting to become a big problem, because we see that young people are more and more eager to get these drinks. The survey, which was conducted on a fairly large volume of young people, took part in this survey of over 2,600 people aged 12 to 20 years, the average age is 15 years, shows that 67% Teenagers consume energy drinks, including 16% Quite often. The study points to possible negative trends in the future, as consumption of these beverages in increasingly younger respondents is increasing significantly. The aid for energy drinks during science is increasing due to composition, mainly due to caffeine and taurine content, and these are not indifferent to health products. Caffeine and taurine are the most common additives for energy drinks. Caffeine is the most commonly used psychoactive substance. It is a plant alkaloid, naturally occurring in plants and grains of many fruits. More than 63 plant species in the world are present Caffeine. That's, among other things. tea shrub leaves, Paraguayan maté, coffee seeds, cocoa and many other plants. Caffeine together with taurine is most commonly used in energy drinks as an ingredient that has a beneficial effect on strength, concentration, reaction time, speed and well-being. Legislative issues. In accordance with the standards passed on by European law on food law, food marketed must not be dangerous to human health and life. It is worth noting that, in accordance with these European acts, the responsibility for food safety at all stages of production, processing and distribution is borne by the owner of a food business operator. There is no definition of energy drinks in food law. The products referred to as energy drinks are mainly characterized by high caffeine content, usually 32 mg per 100 ml of drink, but also with the addition of taurine or B vitamins. The high content of caffeine in these products is detrimental to young people, children, pregnant women, also mothers who are feeding, so on these drinks such slogans, such warnings are placed on the label. The Minister of Health also takes a number of measures to reduce the consumption of energy drinks in children and young people. One of them is the execution of a statutory delegation resulting from the Law of 25 August 2006 on food safety and nutrition. Then we introduced a provision that a group of foodstuffs intended for consumption by children and young people - mainly kindergartens, schools, except adult schools, and other educational establishments - were withdrawn from these facilities. The requirements to be met by foodstuffs used in the mass feed of children and young people have also been regulated. But it's not just that. Last year we also introduced the so-called. sugar levy. It is a fee that applies to drinks with added sweeteners, but also caffeine and taurine. According to the studies carried out by the Market Observatory, sales of carbonated beverages in the first months of 2021 fell by more than 23% and the average price of these beverages increased by 32%. Unfortunately, in this study we also note that this did not affect the sale of energy drinks - of course, there was an increase in their price, but there was no negative impact on sales volume. Are we testing the contents of these substances in drinks? Yes, of course. The Chief Sanitary Inspector shall carry out such tests. There are certain standards that can't be exceeded, and that's under constant monitoring. At the moment, at the Ministry of Health, we are considering possible restrictions on access to high energy drinks for children and young people, as is the case in other countries in Europe. Thank you very much.

First of all, what were the goals of the National Housing Program you're asking about? Housing policy in Poland since 2016 is conducted on the basis of the National Housing Programme adopted by the Resolution of the Council of Ministers in September 2016. The instruments of this policy are constantly adapted to the market situation, but the objectives of the programme set in the 2030 perspective remain unchanged and set the macroeconomic framework for social policy, housing policy in Poland. The main objectives set out in the National Housing Programme are three. The first is to increase access to housing for people with income currently preventing the acquisition or rental of apartments on a commercial basis. The second objective set out in 2016 is to increase the capacity to meet the basic housing needs of people at risk of social exclusion due to low incomes or particularly difficult living conditions. The third objective is to improve housing conditions and the technical state of housing resources and increase energy efficiency. How are these objectives achieved? ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u207	Thank you very much. Mrs Katarzyna Kretkowska, Left.
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u208	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Wielkopolska Uprising was one of only five victorious Polish uprisings. Its effect was to connect the western territories of Poland remaining for 123 years in the Prussian partition to the reborn Republic. In addition to this huge territorial conquest, the uprising also brought the largest weapon of Polish war loot in 1000 years history. How did the uprising turn out to be a success? What distinguishes them from all other Polish uprisings? The uprising lasted short, only 52 days. It killed 2289 insurgents, over 6,000. He was wounded. The insurgents took over the entire Poznań province, except for its northern borders to Bydgoszcz and Piła and the south-eastern ones to Leszno and Rawicz. On 16 February 1919, the peace in Trevira was formally terminated. The insurgents of Greater Poland won a diplomatic victory, culminated in the Treaty of Versailles signed on 28 June 1919, which resulted in almost all Wielkopolska returning to Poland. The fact that the uprising lasted for a short time, sometimes it is a joke that the entire effort of the insurgents was limited to the purchase of platforms to enter the station in Poznań and to welcome the train arriving here, despite the ban, Ignacy Paderewski. That's obviously not true. The brief course of the uprising showed only its effectiveness. The uprising had elements of spontaneous burst, but it was really well thought out, well prepared, in Poznań prepared and abounded in brilliant tactics. Its foundation is the entire period of the acquisition of Prussian and effective resistance from the pressure of culturekampfu, Germanization, economic attempts to subdue German capital. The Great Poles won this longest war of modern Europe with both sacrificial armed breaks, often unjustly forgotten, as well as a stubborn, stubborn strategy of self-modernization and economic strengthening, investing in science, education, culture, and raising the country's agriculture according to state-of-the-art patterns. There were specific features of the Greatpolan: realism, rationalism, sober reckoning with reality, good organization and order. Contrary to stereotypes, this Wielkopolska difference was not the result of taking over some qualities of the Prussian possessor, but was born earlier, still in the Middle Ages. And it was these features of Wielkopolska that caused Wielkopolska since the Middle Ages to be the most urbanized region in the country, the only one who showed constant economic growth despite the wars. This melange of prevention, common sense and love of order, along with deep-rooted patriotism, translated into the nature and course of the Greater Poland Uprising and its victory. In the 19th century this melange resulted in both socio-economic self-modernization, as well as the far-sighted formation of future armed forces in the form of conspiracy teams of scouts or troops During World War I, several hundred thousand Greater Poles were in the German army as a result of their recruitments. They fought on all fronts of Europe, and gained military experience, knowledge of German language, contacts with soldiers and German officers used during the Greater Poland Uprising. On 15 February 1918 the Polish Military Organisation of the Prussian Acquisition was established. At the end of World War I, the Great Poles planned to receive weapons from returning soldiers. Polish soldiers serving earlier in the German army took control of radio stations, telegraph links, entire barracks and arsenals. On 25 December in Buku they took over e.g. The whole train with guns going to Poznan. The laced operation carried out bloodless was so-called. attack on the town hall on November 13, when the Poles interrupted the session of the German Executive Committee of the Council of Soldiers in Poznań. They entered the town hall and replaced four Germans with four Poles, thus gaining control over the committee. Ignacy Paderewski's flaming speech from the balcony of the Hotel Bazar was just a spark for the fuse, which was well prepared and ready to launch. The uprising was full of tactical masterpieces, such as the occupation of the Citadel, the largest fort in Poznań filled by 900 German soldiers and reflected by one Polish telegrapher, Stanisław Jóźwiak, through negotiations and negotiations. As a result of the battle for Sławica, the airport was captured and the planes standing on it were ready to take off. And as a result of the fight for the Zeppelin Hall, 300 battle aircraft and balloons were captured in the Winiars. A total of 200 million German brands were acquired. The uprising had its heroes, but it also had its heroes. Next to famous names such as Ratajczak, Andrzejewski (Dzwonek), General Stanisław Taczak, Józef Dowbor-Muśnicki, we also have the names of the less remembered heroes: Izabela Drwęska, Wanda Nielewska, Maria Kurnatowska, Irena Buszkiewicz, Maria Kulińska. 15 seconds. Even though more than 3,000 were released. publications concerning the Greater Poland Uprising, it is not recorded in national consciousness like other uprisings and does not enjoy such a reflection in our national historical images. The Left Club therefore recommends that the 27th of December, one of the most important and most glorious dates in the history of Polish military and Polish history, become the national day of the victory of the Greater Poland Uprising. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u246	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I present a report by the Committee on Social Policy and Family Affairs on the Government's draft law on family welfare capital, print No 1509, and the report is print No 1601. At its 37th meeting of 17 September 2021, the Sejm shall be held in accordance with Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy for consideration. The bill is a complement to the current family policy and is another element of the building of a coherent system of support and development of Polish families, which has been implemented for years by the Government of Law and Justice. The aim of the bill is primarily to mitigate adverse demographic trends, reduce the poverty rate of families with dependent children, invest in the family, strengthen social human capital, strengthen the instruments to combine parenthood with employment and develop widely understood childcare institutions up to the age of 3. Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! The most important solutions contained in the proposed draft law on family custody capital. A new benefit is introduced for families with children of a maximum amount of 12 thousand. PLN for second and subsequent children aged 12 to 36 months. Applications will be submitted electronically. Support will be independent of family income. The income criterion will not apply and the measures will not be taxed. The benefit will be paid monthly. The monthly amount of support will be PLN 500 for 2 years or PLN 1000 for a year, depending on how parents want to make the declaration. The solution will apply from 1 January 2022. The right to support will also be granted to families who will have a second and subsequent child between 12 and 36 months of age on the date of entry into force of the Act. They will be able to receive pro rata support for the period from 1 January 2022 until their second and subsequent child reaches 36 months of age. The task will be carried out by the Social Insurance Institution. In 2022, about 600,000 could benefit from support. children. An additional element and solution in the act will be to strengthen the care system for children under the age of 3. There will be a co-financing for the stay of the child in the nursery, in the children's club or at the daily caretaker's office of PLN 400 per month. The grant will be intended to cover the child's stay fee in the care institution. This will apply to children who are not covered by family care capital. The support of PLN 400 will be paid directly to the crèche operator, the children's club or the day care provider. The task will also be carried out by the Social Insurance Institution, and this solution will apply from 1 April 2020, with compensation from 1 January 2022. Support is estimated to be covered by ca. 110,000. children. Ladies and gentlemen! High Izbo! At its meeting of 28 September 2021, the Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy discussed the amendments. The amendments were tabled by the parliamentary club Left and around Poland 2050. At the same time, around 2050, two minority applications were submitted. High Izbo! The Committee on Social Policy and Family Policy, after considering this draft law, asks the High House to adopt a procedated draft law. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

It's pretty obvious, and I don't think it needs any further explanation. As far as caring capital is concerned, it is a programme aimed at making it easier for parents to decide on another child. We know, and this results from many studies conducted not only in Poland, but also in the world, from the experience of other countries, that one of the major barriers associated with the birth of further children is the cost, organizational and logistics effort and the costs associated with taking care of the next child. The barriers associated with the birth of the first child - here I respond to this very often emerging charge that we do not include the first children in this instrument - lie completely elsewhere. These are not barriers related to the price of daycare or their availability. The first and basic barrier to the birth of the first child is the apartment. The second very important barrier to the birth of the first child is stable work. As far as the housing problem is concerned, the next item on the agenda is about changes in housing policy, and these changes aim to eliminate, among others. I meet very intensively with different environments, and I do not hide that mostly employers, because employers depend very much on employment, employment policy and sustainability of workers. As for this bill, it can be said that it is another piece in the puzzle, which is to be a coherent puzzle, a coherent policy for families. So this charge is completely untenable to me. When it comes to the question of why this law applies to every other child, another child, it is such that it is incomprehensible, because we should support those who are in a difficult situation who need, not those who have a high income and have two or three children. No, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, no, This is the difference between social policy, which is very much needed and, of course, which we are conducting, and for which many programmes have been introduced, from a family-friendly policy to support families in their efforts to raise their children, regardless of what their income is. And that's why I'm very happy that the 500+ programme is a universal, universal program, i.e. independent of income criteria. In the same way, family care capital is deprived of income because it is not a social program. This is not a programme aimed at reducing poverty in families in the most difficult situation. This is a program that has parents who today have one child or who are thinking about starting a family, helping them decide on these other children, having one, usually small, that requires a lot of care. It's about parents who want to reconcile work and care. I think every woman who experiences motherhood knows exactly what dilemmas we're talking about. We are talking about how difficult it is to combine work with raising children and how difficult it is to decide about each child when you are aware that this requires at least temporary exclusion from the labour market, temporary or longer. This program aims to take into account all needs, all preferences regarding the form of childcare in Polish families. That is not only nursery and institutional care, which, of course, we support and continue to support and develop, but also the recognition that work, which can give fulfillment and sense of life, is, of course, also work at home, at least at this chosen stage. Please note that from many studies, these newest ones, concerning the preferences of women in Poland, it appears that we, women, Polish women, differ in how long we should take a break from motherhood. There are women in Poland who believe that a year is the optimal time, but there are those who believe that 2 or 3 years is the optimal time that should devote only to raising young children. More and more research is also emerging on how important it is to build lasting links for the safety and development of children at a later stage. We support all forms of development. Therefore, this care capital also serves those parents who do not opt for institutional care. I think it's very important. Speaking of institutional care, I would like to say that a little bit in this debate we have escaped the very important issue that this project, apart from introducing family care capital, also introduces what the local authorities have been calling for for for some time, i.e. a permanent mechanism for financing, co-financing and maintaining places in institutional care. According to these changes, each parent of the first child who wants to take institutional care and send the child to daycare will receive a reduction in the daycare fee of 400 PLN. This is a very big change, a very big system change. So far, our budget for the "Maluch+" programme, i.e. 450 million a year, has been allocated to both the creation and maintenance of new places, which is why it was a less stable system. Depending on the year in question, these funds were used differently. Here we respond to the needs of both local authorities, who are making great efforts to maintain nursery facilities, and of course parents, who will thus have a reduced price for this daycare, a crèche fee. I remind you, in the case of the first child, so it's not like the first children don't use it. The question of the sources of funding. The source of funding is, of course, the state budget, i.e. from our joint work as a society, while we are trying to manage the hard-earned funds of Poles in the most optimal and efficient way possible. That is why we have made changes to the 500+ programme to save a lot of money that has been spent on service so far for the 500+ programme. We'll save about 300 million a year. This is one of the sources of funding for this programme. Of course, this program costs much more than 300 million PLN, but 300 million is not small either. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u309	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! Sejm at its 37th meeting on 17 September 2021 pursuant to Article. 39 (1) (a) 2 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm have been submitted by the Government to the Public Finance Commission and the Infrastructure Commission for the purpose of considering the repayment of this loan in connection with the enlargement of the household, print No. 1541, to the Public Finance Commission and the Infrastructure Commission. The Commissions: Public Finance and Infrastructure considered this project on 28 September 2021. High Izbo! Purchase of real estate, primarily the purchase of housing, which is a basic need for many families, often requires borrowing. In our banking system there are rules and rules that generally cover at most 80%, in some cases 90% the value of the property, which means that the remaining 20% of the cost of a given dwelling or house, or ownership rights in multi-building premises are covered by own contribution, i.e. collected by own funds. It turns out that the lack of own resources to cover own contributions is a fundamental barrier for many families, especially young families, to their achievements, in general when it comes to applying for credit, to any dream of their own homes. During the work of the joint committees were presented research conducted under the direction of Prof. Andrzejewski from the University of Economics in Krakow, concerning the preferences of Poles in terms of the form of housing, as well as barriers that hinder the satisfaction of housing needs. Firstly, it turns out that Poles prefer the property of the apartment before the lease in contrast to Western European societies, where the lease is much, much more widespread. In this respect, the project we are working on meets such preferences, such a demand of Poles, because it facilitates the acquisition of apartments, with property rights. Secondly, research shows that the most important barrier reported by families, both large children and young families, is the barrier in terms of getting an apartment or then increasing the mixing due to the enlargement of the family, is precisely the lack of resources for own contribution. In addition to barriers such as too small housing or the lack of peace for children, this own contribution turns out to be a fundamental, most important barrier. Therefore, the proposal put forward by the government exactly responds to these expectations of Poles and actually combines the advantages of all other programmes to date, all the more so because there is no income criterion applied here, i.e. in terms of meeting housing needs, this is a huge progress, a huge step forward. This is a new proposal, it seems, attractive to many families. But this is not just a realization of housing policy. Please note that at the stage of repayment of the loan we are also faced with some support. This applies to these families, those situations when the family grows, new children come into the world. In such a situation it is possible to take over the repayment of the loan - it is called a family repayment - by the bank. As for the second child born in the family, the government takes over 20,000, makes a one-off payment on behalf of the borrower, in the case of the third child and the subsequent children 60,000. zł. These payments will be made from the Government Housing Fund, which is to serve and be created in the Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. The Government Housing Fund will be funded annually from budget resources, from public funds, from around PLN 100 million from 2023 onwards, and will gradually grow every year until PLN 2 billion in 2032. PLN 2150 million - it is estimated that this fund will amount to this amount. These are the most important elements. So this second pillar is, at the same time, the inhibition of negative demographic trends, or rather the incentive to expand the family, through this kind of government proposal. These are two of the most important issues, i.e. the project combines the tasks of housing policy and pro-family policy, combines these two issues, solves these two problems. On the other hand, as regards the very course of the committee meeting, as rapporteur of the committee, I would like to point out that various issues were raised in the discussion, including. the extent to which this programme, i.e. these facilitations, the public support of families in obtaining housing, will increase property prices, the extent to which any increase in interest rates will affect the cost of the credit and threaten the repayment of the credit for many families, creating a danger for themselves or for the banking system itself. I will only mention here that I am surprised at the issue of too low interest rates raised by the opposition, especially in terms of inflation, but you are not saying that it is the low interest rates that the government keeps in great effort, not the government, only the National Bank of Poland... ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u385	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, I have the honour to present to the High House a report on the draft law on combating unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage in trade in agricultural and food products contained in print No 1502. A few words about the proposed bill. This proposal aims to implement into the national legal order Directive 2019/633 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 17 April 2019 on unfair commercial practices between traders in the supply chain of agricultural and food products, hereinafter referred to as Directive 2019/633. The provisions implementing the Directive should apply from 1 November 2021 at the latest. The structure of the agri-food market makes the supply chain of agricultural and food products often differ significantly in the bargaining power between suppliers and buyers of agricultural and food products. In practice, unfair commercial practices take place throughout the food supply chain. At different stages of the agricultural and food supply chain there are different numbers of operators with different operating sizes. In Poland and some other countries of the European Union there are strong, economically consolidated entities in the agricultural raw materials and foodstuffs sector. Differences in the economic potential between the supplier and the buyer may lead to unfair trading practices being imposed by smaller operators. Unfair practices by contract leaders put pressure on the margins and profits of economic operators, which may lead to inadequate allocation of resources or even market disfellowshipping of entities which would otherwise be profitable and competitive. For the proper functioning of the supply chain of agricultural and food products it is necessary to maintain good relations between all its links. Therefore, Directive 2019/633 introduced a common minimum framework within the European Union for applying a uniform approach to unfair commercial practices in the food supply chain. Thus, the draft law contained in print No 1502 is the implementation of this directive into the Polish legal order. Although the provisions of the Act of 15 December 2016 on the prevention of unfair exploitation of contract advantages have been in force in our country since 12 July 2017, the current law does not, for example, apply. a closed list of unfair behaviours, specifying them very generally. Directive 2019/633 introduces, provides for the possibility of introducing rules beyond the scope of the current law, and therefore the government has submitted a draft new law to the High Sejm. On 28 September, at a joint meeting of the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development, following a detailed examination of the draft, the introduction of amendments and a particularly long discussion of the article. The 8th project, which describes cases of unfair exploitation of the contract advantage, the committees welcome the draft submitted by the government and request the adoption by the High Sejm of the draft law annexed to the report, print No 1594. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u387	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present to you my position on the Government's draft law against unfair exploitation of contract advantages in the marketing of agricultural and food products, print No 1502. The first reading of this project took place on 17 September at the 37th meeting of the Sejm and was addressed to the Committee on Economic Affairs and Development and the Committee on Agriculture and Rural Development. On 28 September, these committees met and at their meeting this project was very carefully re-examined. This proposal aims to implement into the national legal order the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 17 April 2019 on unfair commercial practices between traders in the supply chain of agricultural and food products. The structure of the agri-food market highlights unfair commercial practices between suppliers and buyers of agricultural and food products throughout the food supply chain. Such practices are observed in almost all EU countries due to the presence of strong and economically consolidated entities in the distribution sector, and often even in processing, in relation to weaker and fragmented operators producing agricultural raw materials and foodstuffs. For this reason, unfair commercial practices are taking place on the market by these operators, which often leads to the exclusion of weaker operators from the market. For the proper functioning of the food supply chain, it is necessary to maintain good relations between all links. Directive 2019/633, which we are talking about, of 17 April 2019 provides a specific framework for applying a uniform approach to unfair practices in the food chain. The recommended solution is the adoption of a law on countering unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage in trade in agricultural products, and implementation significantly changes the scope of the existing Act of 15 December 2016. In relation to the existing provisions, the draft law introduces new amendments, among others. to extend the definition of agricultural and food products, to modify the buyer's definition, to define unfair practices, to identify disparities in economic potential or to introduce a voluntary penalty procedure. The proposed law guarantees more transparent conditions for operators in the marketing of agricultural and food products. Suppliers, including farmers, and small and medium-sized enterprises producing agricultural and food products will have greater certainty about the functioning on the Community market, including the domestic market, which should translate into pro-development measures, including investment and innovation activities of this socio-professional group. The project includes a number of provisions of competence for the President of the UOKiK, including the imposition of penalties duly catalogued in the various articles, or the possibility of applying provisions of voluntary punishment to the parties to the dispute, i.e. the supplier or buyer. The project allows in accordance with Article. 47 to determine extenuating circumstances when determining the amount of penalties. The entry into force of the proposed law does not preclude a trader from making a claim to a civil court. The project does not involve an increase in expenditure from the state budget or local government units. Since the draft law is in compliance with the provisions of the directive, the parliamentary club supports this law, but, Mr Marshal, makes one amendment. It's about art. Article 8 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1, where the wording for the calculation is as follows: Unfair practices using a contractual advantage shall in particular be regarded as practices consisting of - and in the justification are mentioned all these practices. I won't have time to name them anymore. Thank you kindly. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u395	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Today, we are discussing the introduction of rules to reduce the use of market advantage called contract advantage, the unfair use of contract advantage to impose adverse conditions on suppliers of agri-food products into the chain of shops. What is a contractual advantage and is it prohibited? By economic development, each company strives to have the best position on the market and, in the case of supermarket networks, they gain this position on the market by building new facilities, by increasing the commercial space. Today we already have networks in Poland that have not several dozens, not several hundred, but even several thousand shops. They therefore have a place where the seller's contact occurs - the buyer, i.e. the store shelf and the place where the final consumer reaches and decides which products to buy. Incidentally, the position of the shelf also has a huge impact, so whether it is a shelf within the reach of this consumer, or if it is low, to which he must lean. This shelf is definitely of inferior value and often suppliers are being steered towards that shelf. It could be said that the contract advantage is not an alien phenomenon, but the worst part is that these networks, these shops are trying to unfairly exploit their contractual advantage towards those who deliver to this store, who fight among themselves to have access to the store shelf, which in turn will allow them to reach the consumer. First of all, it is not fair that networks use their advantage to unwarrant huge benefits, i.e. to impose a very high margin, disproportionate to common sense, as well as serious treatment and to consumers and suppliers. But first of all, the unfair practice is that the supplier puts himself in a very difficult situation, because it is limited to accessing this shelf, imposed certain conditions, very adverse, first of all adversely priced, the delivery price is sometimes reduced to such a level that does not guarantee the survival of his company, and in the end, e.g. the farmer who, through this supplier with his product, goes to the consumer is limited to the possibility of achieving financial satisfaction. That's all we need to prevent. The European Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council sets out a number of activities that Member States should in their law apply to this practice of unfair exploitation of the advantage of the contract on the market, thus ending this dominant position on the market. Especially this unfair use occurs in the case of agri-food products, because on the one hand we have this network, which has several thousand shops or several hundred shops, and on the other hand, as partners, we have several thousand or dozens of suppliers who fight, who are economically weak, who are unable to resist the dominance of the great recipient, because he often does not want to talk to them, treats them as the weakest link in the food supply chain from field to table. The European Commission has proposed a series of measures to implement Member States to prevent this. First of all, these are activities that do not allow a weaker entity to be played by a stronger entity. Secondly, the European Commission proposes that Member States should be able to apply a system of penalties if this is the case, i.e. using an unfair contractual advantage. What's in this Polish bill? Unfortunately, there is no action that strengthens the position of a small supplier vis-à-vis the big trading network. There's no strengthening of his position. There are no recorded elements that cannot be found in this agreement, and there is simply a provision that says that you can by voluntary agreements give up the records that are dangerous for the supplier. This unfortunately opens a precedent, which will lead to the fact that networks through negotiation, at a time when they will decide who they will buy from and how much they will force suppliers to agree to certain conditions. Unfortunately, the Act in its present form makes this possible. The bill is good, and that's what we've been talking about today. It is good in terms of the opportunities given to the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, but this is the next stage. This is the stage that applies when a crime has already occurred, and there is no protection against crime in this law. Therefore, we as a club make a few amendments which will be more relevant to the intentions contained in the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council, taking into account the strengthening of the supplier's position vis-à-vis the supermarket network, and not just making the system available to the President of the OMC when there is already a breach of the law and the exploitation of the contractual advantage. It seems to us (the bell) that the introduction of these amendments - they are not complicated, they are allowed by a directive of the European Union, the European Commission and the Council - will make this law more life-saving and more effective in its application. I remember this discussion a few years ago, when I spoke in a similar tone, saying that the mistakes that were made in the bill a few years ago would result in the fact that it will practically have no practical reference. And today, from a perspective of a few years, we can come to the conclusion that unfortunately I was right, because the cases where the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection intervened, imposed a financial penalty for the use of unfair practices, were relatively few. And even if they were, they were ineffective, because so far many of these entities have not paid any penalty. Therefore, Mr. Marshal, I make a number of amendments. I ask the Minister, the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection, to take these amendments seriously. These are not political amendments, but substantive amendments that will allow us, you and, above all, supermarket suppliers to have a better position than today. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u399	Thank you very much. Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! The proposal is intended to implement Directive 2019/633 of the European Parliament and of the Council on unfair commercial practices between traders in the supply chain of agricultural and food products into the national legal order. It should be borne in mind, and I think we all share this view that when it comes to shaping the market in Poland, on the one hand, very strong trading groups, mainly international, but not only our own, domestic and, on the other hand, producer entities, it is full of inequalities, they are characterised by imbalances. Very strong groups, weaker distribution groups, weaker producer groups - this causes a market disturbance and a imbalance in the conclusion of contracts. This shape of the market makes these differences in bargaining power prone to unfair or questionable commercial practices. This was very widely discussed during the committee meeting. However, it is worth remembering that one of the main tasks of the State is to ensure that these conditions of participation, the conditions of the commercial game, the conditions of the market game are equal for all and that there is no artificial imbalance in which the economic potential of one of the operators determines that the other entity contains a very unfavourable agreement on common relations. And this bill is supposed to prevent it. I think it's worth mentioning a few of these articles. E.g. in Article 4, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: 6 sets out a situation in which the use of a contractual advantage is unfair. Art is extremely important. 7, which in the mouth. 2 and 3 specify the difference in potential that can be determined by the situation of taking advantage of this advantage. Art is also important. 8, which defines prohibited practices. On the other hand, the tools for market control are received by the Office of Competition and Consumer Protection and, in fact, will depend on the office on how this right works and whether it will be effective. On the other hand, during the discussion in the committee there was a doubt about the situation of small and medium-sized Polish factories. Poland 2050 therefore presents an amendment (the bell) according to which such a contract between, on the one hand, a broker, not a farmer, but an entrepreneur, who is an intermediary, and, on the other hand, an entrepreneur who is a processor, will be able to be divided into such a structure that 50% payment will be up to 30 days after delivery of the raw material, while the remaining 50% - within 6 months, with interest and margin on WIBOR. It seems that this is fair and very necessary for the market just small and medium-sized Polish factories, which as a result of the adoption of these regulations can simply find themselves in a very difficult financial situation. I'm putting this amendment in the hands of the marshal, and I'm asking for your support. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u425	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! I would like to thank all the parliamentary clubs, Members, for the preliminary support of this law. Such declarations were made here. Thank you for working on this bill earlier. As we know, the draft law aims to implement a directive of the European Parliament in the national legal order. I would like to stress once again that there are provisions in force in our country since 2017 to prevent unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage in the marketing of agricultural and food products. Poland as one of the first Member States introduced such legislation before regulations were adopted at EU level. In relation to the current rules as a result of the extension of the definition of agricultural and food products, accounts concerning buying-in may also be examined, inter alia. feed, live animals, seeds and oil fruits. Let me remind you that the definition of the buyer, which may also be a public body, has also changed. The project defines 16 unfair practices, 10 practices that are strictly prohibited, six practices that are allowed, provided that they have been clearly and unambiguously agreed by the parties prior to their application, i.e. the arrangements between the buyer and the supplier. Ladies and gentlemen! Many of your statements raised the issue of delays in the adoption and implementation of EU legislation. Once again, I will stress that such provisions have been in force in Poland since 2017. We introduce a directive, we extend it more than a directive. The Directive provides for the adoption of national measures by 1 May 2021. Nevertheless, I would like to inform you that this law in Poland is already in operation. The delay results from the fact that at the stage of inter-ministerial arrangements, opinions and public consultations, many comments were submitted which required careful analysis and amendments to the draft law. Public consultation has been extended due to the wide interest of entrepreneurs in the proposed rules. System solutions adopted in our country, i.e. separate provisions relating to the supply chain of agri-food products and separate competition rules have also delayed the implementation of the Directive. It was necessary to coordinate the work on this bill with the work on the draft amendment of the Act of 16 February 2007 on the protection of competition and consumers transposing Directive 2019/1 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 11 December 2018 to confer powers on competition authorities of Member States in order to enforce law more effectively and ensure the proper functioning of the internal market. The rules on unfair commercial practices are well-known and, until the provisions of the draft law enter into force, the suppliers of agricultural or food products are protected by the provisions in force prohibiting unfair exploitation of the contractual advantage. The provisions of the proposed law are to apply in accordance with the Directive from 1 November and all indications are that this deadline will be respected. Among the questions were those concerning the fact that we have no action for small suppliers. Ladies and gentlemen, in our country, the obligation to conclude contracts for the supply of agricultural products is enshrined in the law. Such an agreement must contain essential elements, inter alia. price, payment deadlines. The contract checks shall be carried out by the KOWR and the ratio of its negotiations shall be examined by the OOKiK. One of my fellow Members asked for our country's reply on the European Commission's comments. That's the answer to that. addressed and will be provided to the Member for inspection. I just want to stress that we are not the only country that has not introduced such a directive and repeat that the rules are to apply from 1 November. With regard to strengthening the UOKiK, the President of the institution has been carrying out activities in this area since 2017. A cell was created in the UOKiK, which is dedicated to activities in the field of contract advantage. The President shall take appropriate measures to ensure adequate staff resources for the implementation of his or her competences and activities in this field shall be carried out by the Office. There were repeated questions about the deadline for adoption of the bill, as I have already mentioned. One of your fellow Members asked what programmes and projects the Ministry is planning to secure for farmers in the future when products are delivered. We propose that measures should be designed primarily in the national reconstruction programme, i.e. over EUR 1 billion. Interventions will be addressed to food chain operators, the agri-food sector, including farmers. In general, this €1 billion will be earmarked for processing, distribution and logistics centres, redistribution of food, creation of places for direct sale of agri-food products. We are currently in the process of consulting and creating detailed elements in this area and we are discussing this with representatives of the sector. There were questions about distribution discounts. We have no idea how many entities have taken a stand against such decisions. There is no obligation for us to keep such a register, but the new law gives the President of the Office for Competition and Consumer Protection the opportunity to support farmers in judicial matters in this respect. It was also a question whether these solutions would affect the better functioning of the market and ensure a relationship between the agricultural producer and the recipient of the goods. In 2020 these expenditures amounted to PLN 11.6 billion, mainly investment expenditure. During the pandemic, supply chains were interrupted, they could not have been as efficient as the tender procedures were supposed to have been carried out, so I think it is most appropriate to extend the period of non-extinguishing expenditure. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u543	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On 6 September, the President's regulation on this matter was examined by the High House. The Sejm rejected the request to repeal it, thereby accepting the introduction of an emergency state. On 28 September, the Council of Ministers asked the President to extend the state of emergency for a period of 60 days, in view of the continued special threat to the security of citizens and public order on the border lane. At the same time, the Council of Ministers requested an extension of all existing restrictions. As a reminder, the President's regulation of 2 September introduced the least severe for society and at the same time necessary to address the causes of the emergency. As requested by the Council of Ministers, the basic problem at the Polish-Belarusian border, which is the external border of the European Union, has been intensified in recent weeks by attempts to cross the state border illegally. This involves growing fears and a sense of danger for border residents. In the period from 1 September to today, more than 7 thousand took place. attempts to cross the Polish-Belarusian border illegally. To show the scale I will say that during the whole previous year, which is also during the crisis in Belarus, there were only 120 of them. The nature and extraordinary scale of the migratory pressure have its origins in the planned and planned activities of the Belarusian services aimed at destabilising the situation at the border with Poland and other Member States of the European Union, i.e. Lithuania and Latvia. The Council of Ministers' proposal stresses that the extension of the validity of the state of emergency is essential for the protection of the security of citizens and public order, as well as for the fulfilment of the obligations of the Republic of Poland to protect the external border of the European Union and to ensure stability in the region. High Izbo! On 28 September, the President invited the President of the Council of Ministers and held a meeting with the Minister of National Defence, the Minister of Home Affairs and Administration, the Chief of the Border Guard and representatives of the General Staff of the Polish Army. The President also personally took note of the security situation and the prospects for its development on the border lane during the briefing with the leadership of the Border Guard and the Armed Forces. At the request of the President, as Head of BBN, I am in constant contact with government and service representatives. [[Applause]] As absolutely unacceptable, we treat all provocative and dangerous actions of the Belarusian service towards Polish officers and soldiers to trigger incidents at the border. Many of this information is particularly alarming. At the same time, the President took action in an international forum to present the current security situation in the region, to present the reasons for Poland's application of emergency measures in relation to the protection of the state border, and to build awareness of our partners and allies as to the anticipated long-term deterioration of the security situation at the eastern border of the European Union and NATO. This is mainly due to the strengthening of military relations between the Republic of Belarus and the Russian Federation. The Constitution obliges the President of the Republic of Poland to uphold the security of the State and the integrity of its territory and entrusts it with measures which in emergency situations allow for the elimination of threats. In the view of the President and the Council of Ministers, the reasons for the introduction of the emergency state have not ceased, and further action against the causes of the emergency is possible only through an extension of its duration. According to Art. Point (b) of the first subparagraph is replaced by the following: 2 of the Constitution the extension of the state of emergency may take place only once, with the approval of the Sejm and for a period of not more than 60 days. The prior agreement of the Sejm gives the President the legal opportunity to issue an appropriate regulation on this matter. Therefore, in relation to the request of the Council of Ministers and the circumstances raised therein, the President of the Republic of Poland requests the Sejm's consent to extend the state of emergency to parts of the Podlaskie Voivodeship and Lublin Voivodeship for a further period of 60 days. In the opinion of the President on matters as fundamental as the security of citizens, the widest possible support is needed for the actions taken so far and the proposal presented to the High House. ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u716	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Wolbórz. The earliest historical mention of Wolborz, beside the mention of Łęczyca, Żarnow and Rozprza, is the so-called one. Mogile forgery from 1065, which involves Bolesław Brave's giving to the monastery in Mogile... Wolbórz is mentioned as a castle town, which is the seat of the castle of the province of Lęczycka. This fact is confirmed by the source record in the bull by Pope Innocent II. The location of the Wolborsky castle and its rapid development were decided by the location on a route important in the early Middle Ages of the Gniezno-Kiev trade route. The privilege of Pope Eugenius III, issued in Rome in 1148 and then the papal bull, confirms the donation of the Wolbor castle along with the adjacent villages to the bishop of Wrocław Werner. In 1148 the construction of the church of St. Santa in Wolborz. In 1215 the Provincial Synod, led by Archbishop Henryk Kietlicz, took place here, discussing the needs of the church, the country and problems related to the Lateran Council appointed by Pope Innocent III. In addition to the clergy, the Synod was gathered by the Dukes of the District of Poland. In 1228 Duchess Grzymisław - the widow of Leszek Biały, approved the borders of the Wolbor castle, which are based on the rivers Pirsno, Grabówka, Wolborka and Tesni. In 1273, the Duke of Sieradzki Lęczycki Leszek Czarny gave Wolborz city rights. He allowed bishops to settle on German law Wolbórz and villages belonging to Wolborz, and the city of Wolbórz with a stone wall, brick or palisade. The people of Wolborz received tax and danin exemption. The settlers got the squares and after building the houses they were free of taxes for 6 years. The city also received its own courts. From 1377 comes the first change of the Wolbor castle. It was erected in a defensive place in the fork of the Moszczanka River. A few years later, he was identified by Janek of Czarnkov as a fortress. At the end of the 14th century, history confirmed the value of the city, as it ran through important so-called. Lithuanian route from Małopolska through Mazowsze to Lithuania. Since the beginning of the 15th century, the political and strategic importance of Wolborz was growing. In 1410 the concentration of troops took place in Wolborz for an expedition against the Teutonic Knights, in accordance with the order of King Władysław Jagiełła. The king arrived in the city on the day of St. John the Baptist. He was there on 26 June 1410 to go to Grunwald for a victory fight. King Władysław Jagiełło stood in Wolbor 16 times, among others. in July 1420 He accepted the Czech MEPs who offered him the crown of the Czech Republic. In 1461, Kazimierz Jagiellonian was here. In Wolborz there were often Italian bishops, and some, for example. Jacob of Sienna - almost constantly. The 16th and 17th centuries are a period of greatness in the city. Numerous national and church dignitaries hosted at the Wolbor castle on their way to the Sejm to Piotrków Trybunalski. In 1503, Andrzej Frycz Modrzewski was born in Wolborz. Between 1553 and 1567 he served as a governor and laid the foundations for organized fire suppression. Modrzewski died of the plague in the fall of 1572. In the 16th century Wolbórz belonged to the largest cities of the Sieradzki and Lęczycki voivodship. He counted about $4,000. residents. Since the beginning of the 17th century. Volborz begins to decline, many times experienced by natural disasters. In 1618 the city was haunted by a great fire and pestilence. On 13 June 1766 the Wolbor castle is on fire. Bishop Antoni Ostrowski is attempting to lift Wolborz. It completes the renovation of the town hall, the reconstruction of the church of St. Nicolas, pave the streets of the city and the market and build a new representative seat of bishops in the south of the country. The building of the residence lasted for the years 1768 and 1773, and the main designer was the Royal architect Francesco Placidi. In 1815 the bishops of Wolbor were confiscated by the Tsar, and the bishops' palace was dedicated to military barracks. Since 1843 the critical ratio of the Tsarian authorities has been increasing. In May 1870 the mayor of Wolborz received a letter from the head of Piotrkowski in Russian, in which the city is deprived of its rights. In the last 20 years, city rights have been restored. On 1 January 2011, the Council adopted Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the Wolbórz became a city again. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u717	Mr Patrick Wicher, Law and Justice. There's no envoy. Mrs Anna Dąbrowska-Banaszek, Law and Justice. Why don't I get you to make a statement on the record?
ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u718	Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Chelm - my home town is picturesquely located on the Uherka river, approx. 20 km from the border with Ukraine. It is famous for its unique chalk undergrounds in Europe. It is a unique world-wide maze of underground corridors forged in a chalk rock, whose deposits lie abundantly beneath the city's surface. They stretch under the entire old city on several floors. They are a remnant of the extraction of rich chalk deposits. The extraction of chalk for economic purposes took place already in the Middle Ages. The Chelmen profited greatly from this activity. The chalk was used, among others. in construction, ceramics, paint industry. The Chelmians often sent shipments of raw materials to distant cities, e.g. to Krakow for the Krakow Academy. The extraction was interrupted in the 19th century when hollow pavements began to endanger the safety of the inhabitants. The hollow hollow corridors underneath the city used to serve as cellars for the inhabitants. In 1944 the mine was entered in the register as a third-class monument, a unique remnant of old-Polish and chalk mining in Europe. Chełmski Underground Kredowe was made available for sightseeing as early as 1911, but during World War II the tour was stopped by the occupation authorities. At that time, the underworld served as a refuge for the boasters. After many reconstruction and conservation works, the underground was again made available to tourists since 1974. To this day, a section of the mine with unique forms of chalk karst and eagle relief has been preserved. On January 25, 1995, the decision of the voivodship conservationist of monuments was made by a multi-level complex of mining selections located under the old town of Chełm. The Creed Underground in Chełma received many distinctions and diplomas. The most valuable one is the "Przebiśnieg 97" - for the most interesting initiative creating a modern tourist offer in Poland and the "Beautiful Poland" - a diploma for creating beauty that can be boasted of in front of the world. In 2006 the chalk underground was awarded the certificate of the Polish Tourist Organization in the 4th edition of the nationwide competition. In the underground there is a constant temperature, independent of the weather outside, and it is +9°C, while humidity varies between 70 and 85%. The tourist route is approx. 2 km long, located under the central part of the old town. On its trail you can see an exhibition about archaeology, chalk mining and the history of the city, encounter a bat and meet the master of the underground - the spirit of Bieluch. I cordially invite you to visit Chełm and visit our unique chalk undergrounds. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
