According to data from the Railway Transport Office in 2020, 425 accidents were reported on railway lines, including six serious accidents, i.e. 19%. less than 2019 in which 525 accidents occurred, of which 4 serious. Of course, these data were affected by the restrictions associated with the epidemic period, which contributed to a significant drop in demand for transport, which resulted in less traffic on tracks and roads. It is worth noting that only slightly more than 20 events are responsible for the railway system alone. The rest are accidents on rail and road crossings and during illegal crossings. Construction of collision-free crossings, for example through the construction of railway viaducts, can significantly reduce accidents involving trains, cars and pedestrians. In addition, this will increase traffic flow by reducing the number of road-rail crossings. An example can be the district of Iławski in Warmia-Mazurskie voivodship. The inhabitants of Iławy and representatives of local government units of the district of Iławski call for increased safety of drivers and pedestrians during the E65 railway line on the Warsaw-Gdynia route. Road users face a number of obstacles related to rail crossings, in particular, congestion resulting from leaving the barrier during train crossings. Iława as a 40-thousand-dollar city of high tourist value has constantly been abandoned. Meanwhile, in the very center of the city there is a passage paralyzing traffic throughout the city. Pendolino trains run through this city with a lot of intensity. Intercity, Tanich Railway Lines and Regional Transport should also be added to this. In addition, it is also a route, which is often driven by freight warehouses towards ports in Tricity. Every one of these trains has to cross the city. As a result, railroads block the main street several times an hour, which makes it hard to say of any fluid movement in Iława, especially in the afternoon. The construction of the collision-free crossing at this point is all the more justified because Iława can be one of the cities through which the S5 expressway will be led. In order to ensure adequate road safety for users, as well as to improve the communication system in the city recommended by the above-mentioned solution. The problem would be to build two collision-free road-rail crossings. One of these objects should be erected at the intersection of the voivodship road No 536 with the railway line E65 Warszawa - Gdynia at ul. Wyszynski in Iława. The second expected construction of the collision-free road-rail crossing concerns the intersection of the national road no. 16 with the railway line E65 Warszawa - Gdynia at the exit from Iława towards Kisielice. These two crossings are key points on the road map of the Iławski district and the Warmia-Mazurskie voivodeship itself. The first one is located in the city center. As Iława is located on a very busy railway route connecting the Tricity with Warsaw and Krakow, the rate of leaving the barrier is very high, which paralyzes traffic in the city, creating numerous traffic jams. In turn, the second of the intersections is located on the national road no. 16, i.e. on an important transport route. This road connects the A-1 motorway with the S7 expressway, therefore it is a strategic road for the Warmia-Mazury voivodeship. In view of the new financial perspective of the European Union's budget for 2021,2027 I call on the government to create a special programme of collision-free construction (the bell) of road-rail crossings... Mr President, please proceed to the end. ...which will help to improve the safety of the inhabitants of Iława and Kisielic in the Ławski district.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u744	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! This year the 90th anniversary of the creation of the Municipal Public Library in Tomaszów Lubelski is celebrated. In 1929, the city council decided to organize a library accessible to all residents in order to develop its readership. The official opening of the library, which was located on the floor of the new exhibition halls, took place on 28 January 1930, and Maria Boska became the first librarian. During World War II, the Germans closed the library. In 1946 it resumed its activity, it had 2102 volumes at the time. By the end of 1948 29 library points were established in the surrounding villages. In 1965, a branch for children and young people was launched in the care of the youngest readers. In 1984, the library, which has so far operated in different locations and premises, was moved to the Kombatant House, where it exists and operates until today. In 2011, Tomasz Zamoyski, chancellor of the Great Crown, Kiev Voivode, II Zamoj Head of the Zamoj Region, founder of the city of Tomaszów became the patron of the Municipal Public Library. For a number of years, many interesting projects have been carried out in the library for the benefit of the residents of the city and the county. Between 2013 and 2016, more than 2,300 thousand were acquired. zł from the program of the library infrastructure for the general renovation of the building and for the purchase of modern equipment. Thanks to these funds, the library gained a distinctive decoration and became accessible to people with disabilities. The purchase of new publications is systematically financed. Employees participate in many actions promoting reading. There are a number of competitions for children and adults. A public reading of the Bible is organized each year at the initiative of the library staff. As part of the programme, the library carried out a number of projects, including: starting the construction of monuments called memorial columns, which are devoted to the most important events from the history of Poland and the city. On this very important day, special thanks are due to all the staff of the library. It is thanks to your personal commitment that all plans and intentions have been realized. Thanks to the city's public library. Tomasz Zamoyski became a leading cultural institution not only in the district of Tomaszów, but also in the region. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2021-09-30-sejm-38-2.u750	The inhabitants and organizations of Silesia Cieszyński who care about the preservation of the region's heritage pay attention to the insufficient protection of material elements (border posts) of the historical border between Silesia Prussian (Górny Śląsk) and Austrian (Silesia Cieszyński) from the years 1742/1918, which are located in the municipality of Strumień and the communes of Pawłowice in the Śląskie voivodeship. For many inhabitants of Silesia Cieszyński, these border posts represent a great historical value of this region. The historical circumstances of this case begin with the Silesian wars, which were also commonly known as the wars for Austrian succession and the seven-year-old war (I Silesian war - 1740 and 1742, II Silesian war - 1744 and 1745, seven-year-old war - 1756 and 1763). These events have fundamentally changed the geopolitical face of Silesia. The conflict between the Austrian Empire and the Kingdom of Prussia ended the peace contained in Hubertsburg. By its power, Silesia, which has so far been part of the Habsburg Empire, has been divided between Austria and Prussia. The Austrians managed to save only a fraction of their previous state of possession, as only 10% territories prior to the outbreak of wars - Silesia Cieszyński and Silesia Opawski. The loss of a significant part of Silesia by the Austrian Empire was one of the reasons for which the Austrians came to Prussia and Russia with the initiative of the first partition of Poland. In this way the dividing line between Prussian and Austrian Silesia was established. In the area of today's Pszczyńskie and Cieszyńskie district, the border ran along a much older line of division of Piast district dukes. This border had a huge impact on the development of the economy, culture, and even the Silesian bustle and physically disappeared only in 1922 after the connection of Upper Silesia to the II Polish Republic. At the border of today's municipality Strumień and the municipalities of Pawłowice are preserved souvenirs of this history, which is often mistakenly associated with the period of partitions. These are border marks, some of which are still in relatively good condition. Judging by the heraldic markings, they were already placed at the turn of the 18th and 19th centuries. Most of the poles have remained without proper conservation protection for decades, their status is not regulated, they are located on land of different legal status, some have been moved from the original location, possibly some physically ceased to exist. Without decisive intervention, the remaining ones can be lost forever in the coming years. The chance for future generations to also have contact with streaming and pavlovice poles is to include these border marks in the register of historical monuments of the National Heritage Institute and to create an educational path on their trail, as I requested a few days ago in an appropriate interpellation to the Minister of Culture, National Heritage and Sport.

Until now, funds have been transferred, among others. to: - reconstruction of the municipal road along with reconstruction of the reachers in Głobina - over PLN 2.5 million, - reconstruction and extension of the streets: Crafts, Brzozowa and Miejska in Siemianice - more than 400 thousand. zł, - construction of multi-purpose field in Krępa Słupska - 360 thousand. zł, - reconstruction of the street. Zygmunt Augusta in Słupsk - over PLN 4.5 million, - construction and equipment of the kindergarten in Włynkówk - nearly PLN 3 million, - the extension of the primary school. Jan Kochanowski in Kończew - 700,000. zł, - reconstruction of the street. Convenient in Siemianice - over PLN 1 million, - renovation of the stadium at ul. Green in Słupsk - more than PLN 0.5, - reconstruction and expansion of the street. Walking in Siemianice - almost PLN 2 million, - construction of a locker room baseball field in Sicewice - close to PLN 0.5 million, - construction of the street. Polna in Kobylnica - 600 thousand. zł, - construction of the street. Rainbow in Bolesławice - over PLN 0.5 million, - reconstruction of the district road No 1130G which is access to S6 junctions - close to PLN 3.5 million. Pomerania is gaining thanks to new government investments, but, which must be emphasized in particular, due to good cooperation with the local government, these investments can be implemented quickly and effectively. I am very pleased with every investment made in my region. It is very important for me to take care of the development of Pomerania and its inhabitants, so from this point of view I would like to thank you very much for the opportunity to obtain additional government resources to carry out important projects.
